breaks. **Storage (#178, the lead):** the records moved to
`hazel-play-history-<kid>`; days an older build's tab leaves under
`hazel-play-days-<kid>` are folded in and that key cleared, so neither
writes over the other. **Reads (#176):** an unread row is read again after
15 s, then twice as long after each failure up to 5 min (back to 15 s on a
read, load or clear); the in-flight flag belongs to the load, so a hung read
for the last kid no longer stops the next kid's timer. 📈 remembers for the
session what a database without 0026 lacks (one round of reads after the
first miss). Switch player and opening 👪 send the waiting play at once; two
windows used in turn no longer count one minute twice (`claimMinute`, a
55 s per-kid stamp); removing the kid who's playing puts their game away;
the notice names the level each topic reached; 0026's comment says a tie
takes the newer tally whole (kept, #179). **Not built:** a keep-first
exception for a grown-up's birth-date fix (logged #183, the lead); a `since`
row for kids idle at deploy (the page then claims no "counted from", every
word stays literally true, and their first new day starts the record);
a timeout on the profile read (#184); `CONSENT_VERSION` waits (#124). Tests
2411 → 2427, the full suite green; lint, `tsc -b`, `build:web` with the
placeholders (`ProgressPage` 37.0 → 39.1 KB, 12.75 KB gz); 26 migrations +
20 SQL tests on Postgres 16, the bundle twice; 36 of 36 mutations caught;
the stubbed app at 320×568, 375×667, 740×360 touch and 1024×768 keyboard.
Deploy: as the PR (re-run `apply_all_migrations.sql`; 0026 changed only in a
comment), no edge function.

### 2026-10-21 — Progress history: XP over four weeks, minutes played, answers per topic, starts on the profile, a profile read again (#169, #170, #172, #173, #174, #176)
The owner: finish the logged-for-later items. 📈 Progress says only what the
database stores, and it stored the days played alone. **Migration 0026**
stores the rest: each `play_days` row keeps the day's `xp`, active `minutes`
and per-topic `topics` tallies (`{a, r, l}`: asked, right, the level),
noted only through `note_play_history(kid, days jsonb)` — security definer,
UTC, the family's own kid, 7 back to 1 ahead (other days skipped, never an
error), at most 31; values clamped and junk dropped; the larger total kept
for a day, per topic the tally with more answers (a tie takes the new one);
each day it writes is marked `noted_play` (#181, added by the follow-ups).
Days are kept and readable **35 days** (was 14), and `note_play_days` is
redefined with the same tidy-up, so an old tab can't delete history at two
weeks. `play_history_since` keeps each kid's first day ever noted
(backfilled, never tidied, gone with the kid). `profiles.skill_starts` is
the kid's play (on `profiles_kid_play_columns()`, no 👪 window); its trigger
keeps the first start per topic and drops anything but a whole level 1–10
and age 3–18, for every writer — and moves each start's age, never its
level, when a grown-up corrects the kid's birth date (#183, the
follow-ups). **On the device** each kid's day is a
record (`lib/playDays.ts`): the XP (`addXp` → `addDayXp`, even while the
row is unread), active minutes (`lib/activeMinutes.ts`: a minute counts with
the game on screen, the tab in view and a key, pointer or touch in it) and
right / asked per topic with the level (`profileStore.noteAnswer` from
every `QuestionCard`'s final pick, `QuizRound` and a timed-out defend; bank
questions only). Today's record stays and goes again whole as it grows, at
most every two minutes (a new day, a profile load, back online and a hidden
tab at once). **The lead's call:** a day's minutes go up only once the day
counts as played (XP, a round, a battle… — `recordActivity`); a kid who
only walks the world has those minutes kept on the device unsent, so the
week, the streak and "last played" agree (#177). **The page:** #169 "📊 XP,
the last 4 weeks" — daily columns, a row per week (oldest first, one scale,
its dates and XP in words), the best day said, every day in a folded table
twin; columns, not the planned line, since many days have no play and a
line draws through them; a day noted without its play (an older app, a
0025 row) is a grey "played, XP not recorded" dot, never 0. #172 "⏱️ About
40 minutes of play in the last 7 days" at a glance (to the nearest 5, "a
few minutes", "about 1 hour 20 minutes"), each day's minutes in the week's
spoken text and the table. #173 each topic "Last 7 days: 7 of 10 right · Up
1 since last week" (or Down / Same, against the latest level noted 8–14
days back); 5+ answers under half right joins 💡, grouped by why. #170 days
before the first noted read "not recorded", with "Counted from Monday 19
October — earlier days weren't recorded." under the week and the chart; the
draft privacy notice names the day notes and their five weeks, and the
topic starts (`CONSENT_VERSION` waits for the lawyer, #124). #174 a topic's
start is noted on the profile too (written with the level, still in the
save's `topicStarts`), carried over from the save on load, merged as a
union; 📈 reads the profile's first. #176 an unread row is asked for again
every 15 s and when back online, and a level moved meanwhile goes onto the
real one as a step (a topic the row never had keeps its level and gets its
start). **Without 0026** each side falls back: the app sends the days alone
to `note_play_days` and keeps the rest on the device (a week back) for a
session that has it, and reads and writes profiles without `skill_starts`
(42703 / PGRST204, for the session); 📈 reads as before (no XP chart, ⏱️ or
answers). Tests 2348 → 2411, the full suite green; lint, `tsc -b`,
`build:web` with the placeholders — `ProgressPage` 26.7 → 37.0 KB (12.1 KB
gz), main 1,380.5 KB (403.2 gz); 26 migrations + 20 SQL tests on Postgres
16 (new `progress_history.test.sql`, which re-applies 0026 over live data
with `\ir`; `play_days`, `grown_ups_gate` and `close_account` updated), the
bundle applied twice, 44 loosenings of 0026 all caught; client 38 of 41
mutations caught (3 equivalent); dashboard 21/21; the stubbed app at
320×568, 375×667, 740×360 touch and 1024×768 keyboard (75 checks). Logged:
#177–#182. Deploy: re-run `apply_all_migrations.sql` (or `db push`), either
order with the app, no edge function (Commands).
### 2026-10-22 — The home-screen app review round 1: no white flash, the FAQ says how, the keyboard to check (#5)
`/saas-code-review` (nothing found) and `/saas-ux-review` (3 medium) of
PR #90; the owner said fix all.
- **No white flash at launch:** the page had no background until the
  game's script (1.37 MB, 398 KB gzipped) ran, so a launch went dark
  splash → white → the game. `index.html` now has
  `html:has(#root:empty) { background-color: #1e1b4b; }` — the splash's
  colour, and only while `#root` is empty, so no screen's backdrop changes
  (a browser without `:has` shows white, as before).
- **The grown-ups FAQ says how:** "What does my child need?" gains a
  paragraph: open the game (a `data-play` link), then Share → Add to Home
  Screen (iPhone, iPad) or ⋮ → Add to Home screen (Android); iPhones and
  iPads ask to sign in once more (their home-screen app keeps its own
  storage). Adding the public page itself gives a plain shortcut to it: the
  manifest is the game's.
- **Kept full screen; the keyboard is TC-2114's to check:** Android may
  not resize a `display: fullscreen` window for the on-screen keyboard, which
  could hide a field low on the page. That can't be tried without a phone;
  if it does, `standalone` (status bar shown, keyboard resizes) is the
  fallback (ISSUES #5).

Checked: `favicon.test` (+1), lint, `build:web`, and headless Chromium:
with the script blocked the page is `rgb(30, 27, 75)` (was transparent),
with it Sign in shows over the page as before; the FAQ at 320, 375 and
1024 px, its link to `/play/`, no sideways scroll.

### 2026-10-22 — The home-screen app: Add to Home Screen opens the game full screen (#5)
The owner asked for it after asking whether the game plays on a tablet or
phone (it does: the d-pad and ✋ on any touch screen, #37 / #127; only touch
typing needs a keyboard, #165).
- **`public/manifest.webmanifest`:** `id`, `start_url` and `scope` `/play/`
  (the game never navigates out of it), `display: fullscreen` (Android hides
  the status bar too; iOS takes it as `standalone`), `#1e1b4b` for the theme
  and the splash, and three icons: Ember's egg at 192 and 512 px (`any`,
  about 80%, like the 180 px one) and a 512 px `maskable` one whose egg fits
  inside the middle circle (40% of the size from the centre) Android crops
  to. Root paths, like the favicon: the game is built with base `/` and only
  its page moves (`build:web`); Vite copies `public/` as it is.
- **`index.html`:** `<link rel="manifest">`; `mobile-web-app-capable` and
  `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` (iOS before 16.4 reads only the meta;
  Chrome warns about the Apple one alone); `apple-mobile-web-app-title`
  "Hazel Quest"; status bar `black`, not `black-translucent` — that draws the
  page under the status bar, and nothing pads the top for it (the viewport
  has no `viewport-fit=cover`).
- **Icons:** `site/tools/prep-assets.py` draws them (`icon(…, fill=)`)
  straight into `public/` — the public pages don't link the manifest. A
  rerun rebuilt every other image byte for byte (TC-1259).
- **No service worker** (`vite-plugin-pwa` stays unadopted): Chrome no
  longer needs one to install, the game can't play offline anyway
  (questions, saves and sign-in are online), and a cached old version is the
  stale-tab trouble the save version guard exists for (#101i). Offline stays
  open in #5.
- **Worth knowing:** on iOS a home-screen app keeps its own storage, apart
  from Safari's, so a family signs in once more inside it (saves come from
  the server, `saveStore`); an email link (a reset, a confirmation) opens in
  the browser, not the app. With no browser bars, the game's own 🔄 Refresh
  and Reload (`OutdatedScreen`, `ErrorBoundary`) are the way back from an
  update or a crash — they were already there.

Checked: `favicon.test` (4 new tests), lint, `build:web` (the manifest and
icons at `dist/`'s root, the tags in `dist/play/index.html`), and headless
Chromium on `vite preview` at 375×667 touch: the manifest served as
`application/manifest+json`, parsed with no errors, no installability
errors. Not yet tried on a real phone or tablet (TC-2114).

### 2026-10-21 — Parent dashboard: a crash-card test that doesn't race the live region (#29)
CI's `test` job failed on PR #88 with "Found multiple elements with the text: Something went wrong showing progress…": `GrownUpsArea.test`'s crash-card test looked for the card's paragraph with `findByText`, but the area's live region says the same words in a `<p>` of its own, and on CI's runner its effect had already run. The test now waits for the paragraph outside the `role="status"` region, and for the live region's line, so either order passes. Test only; 2348 green, lint + `tsc -b` clean.

### 2026-10-21 — The parent dashboard: 📈 Progress for each kid in 👪 Grown-ups, and the days they played (#29)
Parents could see nothing of their kid's progress. Now each kid's row in 👪
Grown-ups has **📈 Progress** (`ProgressPage`, lazy-loaded; shaping in
`lib/progress.ts`), a toggle per kid at the top, the family read once (three
reads). Per kid: the player level as the one big number, XP and how far to
the next level (and that levels never make questions harder); the streak (0
once a day was missed, best kept) and last played in words; the last 7 days,
read day by day; every topic's question level as "Math: level 6 of 10" with
one line on what it means for their age (the question writer's bands),
topics no round has moved grouped at their starting level; the story — the
act reached, that act's and earlier crystals, the Spire, their own 🚩 goal,
trophies by name, nothing about acts not reached; the Library's missed
questions in words (question, right answer, what they picked or that time
ran out, why, topic; newest first, three then "Show all"); touch typing.
"Hasn't played yet", loading and error states (`plainErrorOr`, Sign in again;
raw errors logged). Never the birth date, a PIN or an id. Reads need no 👪
window, but the page is behind the gate and goes back to it after 15
minutes. **History, not invented:** "last 7 days" needed data that wasn't
stored, so **migration 0025** adds `play_days` (kid, day; RLS: the family
reads its own kids' days; only `note_play_days` writes — the family's own
kid, adding days from a week back to a day ahead, kept 14 days; cascade on
removal) and `lib/playDays.ts` notes a new day from `recordActivity`, kept on
the device until sent; XP over time isn't stored and stays out (#169).
**Charts:** plain HTML/CSS by the dataviz method — a hero number, stat tiles,
meters, 10-cell level bars, a 7-cell week — one sequential blue on its own
track plus the de-emphasis grey, validated with the palette script on the
card's white (the 👪 area is a white card, no dark mode); values always in
words beside the bars. **No recharts** (one bar chart alone is ~106 KB
gzipped) and no router. `content/badges.ts` (`badgeInfo`) is now shared with
the menu's shelf (and ignores `constructor`-style ids); the 👪 row's three

### 2026-10-21 — Grown-ups review round 2: a reset link really ends the wait, a check the server can tell apart, Close account waits too, and the wait says what works (#167, #124d, #168)
`/saas-code-review` (1 high, 2 medium, 3 low) and `/saas-ux-review` (2
medium, 6 low + also noted + a hand-over) of PR #87, all fixed but one
(below). **The reset link (high):** GoTrue's implicit flow signs every email
link in with `amr: otp`, so `allow_password_change` refused on the reset page
and every reset *started* the 24-hour wait. Now `grown_up_reset_sign_in()`
ties it to the reset email itself: `recovery_sent_at` set and its token used,
an email-link `amr` entry (`otp` / `recovery` / `magiclink`) from that email,
under an hour old, made with the sign-in, no password change since — a
device signed in by its sign-up link long ago doesn't pass. CI now plays
GoTrue's writes (otp, `recovery_sent_at` / `recovery_token`, the other
sessions signed out in the change's transaction). **A check the server tells
apart (medium):** the device asks first (`askForPasswordCheck` →
`ask_grown_up_check`, `asked_at`), and a check must be a password sign-in
made after that ask (`grown_up_check_refusal(p_target)`, an allow-list), so
the device's own older sign-in never passes, noted or not;
`allow_password_change` from a check and `delete_family` both need the
recorded check row. **Close account honours the wait (medium):**
`delete_family` refuses during it (HQ002 `password_changed`),
`delete-account` answers 403 `password_changed` with `until`
(`passwordWaitDb`), and the app says it calmly on the page — the pre-close
unlock's refusal too — instead of the "locked again" dialog. **Low:** the
docs say "require current password" skips any email-link sign-in for its
life; the allowance is for one sign-in (`allowed_session`), 60 s, dropped
when the save fails (`dropPasswordChange`), read by a deferred constraint
trigger at commit; a network failure no longer marks the current password
wrong (`currentPasswordWrong`). **UX:** in the wait the gate and dialog drop
the box and "Open" — "Use my grown-up PIN" (beside "Didn't change it? Reset
it now") or "Email me a reset link" is the main button, and once the email
is on its way the box comes back for the new password; the words are calm
and amber, claim nothing about the old password, and say what works
(`passwordWaitMessage` `doing`: open / change / close); "today / tomorrow at
…" (`whenWords`); the setup screens' "Not now" speaks to the grown-up and the
kid; a player whose PIN didn't save keeps the "Add a player" title, its line
comes into view ("Tap Save to set their PIN."), and a first player no longer
flashes "Who's playing?" — the setup page says what happened
(`pinNotSavedFor`); signed out is one "Sign in again" with an honest lead;
back at the gate after 15 minutes it says why (`LAPSED_NOTE`); the sign-in
page's link notices are read out (set a beat after mount). **Also noted:**
one wording for an ended sign-in (`DeviceSignedOutError`); the reset email's
next step; the expired-link sentence in two; Done waits while signing out.
Found rendering: the wait notes on Change password and Close account landed
below a phone's fold — they come into view now. **Not built:** Tab wrapping
through `<body>` in `ModalLayer` (shared by every world overlay, as a native
dialog does; ISSUES #167). Tests 2210 → 2227; 24 migrations + 18 SQL tests on
Postgres 16; 41 loosenings of 0024, 38 caught and 3 equivalent (redundant
guards); the function against a stand-in 25/25 (the previous version 22/25);
`deno check` of both functions; lint, `tsc -b`, `build:web`; the stubbed app
at 320×568, 740×360 touch and 1024×768 (207 checks). Deploy order unchanged
(Commands).

### 2026-10-21 — Grown-ups review round 1: a new password can't open 👪 behind the grown-up's back, only a real check names a sign-in, dialogs that fit and say where they are (#167, #124d, #168)
`/saas-code-review` (1 critical, 1 medium, 4 low) and `/saas-ux-review` (8 +
also noted + 3 handed to code review) of PR #87, all fixed but one (below).
**The password (critical):** GoTrue lets any sign-in under a day old set a
new password with no code and no current password, and a new password is a
fresh sign-in that could open 👪. Now the app always sends
`current_password` (`changePassword`), the owner turns on "require the
current password" (Commands), and whatever the settings **0024 notes every
password change** (a trigger on `auth.users`, `grown_up_password_changes`):
one the app announced first (`allow_password_change` — the current password
checked just now, or a reset link's sign-in within the hour; one change, 10
minutes) is fine, any other means no password opens 👪 for 24 hours
(HQ002 `password_changed` with the time; the grown-up PIN still does, and a
new password from a reset link ends the wait). The reset page and Change
password announce theirs; the gate and Change password say until when
(`passwordWaitMessage`); the done page says a pending email change was
cancelled. **The minute after a sign-in (medium):** the app notes each
sign-in it runs on (`note_device_session`, `grown_up_sessions`); only a
password check under a minute old that the app never ran on may name a
sign-in, one only (`grown_up_check_refusal`), and `delete_family` takes the
caller's session (`p_caller`) and refuses a device's or none (HQ002
`reauth_required`). **Low:** the profiles gate lists the kid's columns
(`profiles_kid_play_columns()`; any other column is a grown-up one, and a CI
check fails until a new column is classified); Close account says "Sign in
again" at once when this device's sign-in is gone (`device_signed_out`)
instead of re-asking the gate; `passwordCheckClient()` is a new client per
check (no shared sign-out racing the next); `delete-account` on a database
without 0024 refuses (`database_out_of_date`, 503, nothing deleted) instead
of deleting the login alone. **UX:** Escape on the dialog puts focus back on
the button pressed (`ModalLayer` `restoreFocusOnEscape`, opt-in; the world
rule stays); on a short screen the PIN pad's ways out sit beside it
(`PinPad` `beside`) and "Not now" fits at 320×568; the words follow where it
is (`inArea`: "locked again" only in 👪 Grown-ups, "Just checking it's a
grown-up" on a setup screen, and "Not now" there asks for a grown-up —
`grownUpsLockedMessage`); `no_session` says this device's sign-in ended
with "Sign in again" (gate, dialog, Change password); an email-change link
that comes back says what's left on "Who's playing?" (`emailLinkNoticeFrom`;
an expired one asks again in 👪 Grown-ups); "Forgot it?" is "under the
password box" on the change forms; the dialog says what to do once; the
change pages say why the current password. **Also noted:** Sign out says
"Signing out…" and waits (`SignOutButton`); "the link in each email";
👪's list goes back to the gate once the 15 minutes are up
(`grownUpsStore.openedAt`); focus on the area's and the first player's
titles; the gate's Back fits at 740×360. **Handed over:** a player added
whose PIN didn't save makes the form "Change …" (`KidAddedWithoutPin`, no
duplicate); a right grown-up PIN on an ended sign-in opens nothing and says
so (`signed_out`). **Not built:** cancelling a pending email change (GoTrue
has no call for it; a new change replaces it). Tests 2112 → 2210; 24
migrations + 18 SQL tests (new `grown_ups_password.test.sql`) on Postgres
16, 33 loosenings of 0024 each caught; the function against a stand-in 20/20
(the previous version 7/20); `deno check` of both functions; lint, `tsc -b`,
`build:web`; the stubbed app at 320×568, 740×360 touch and 1024×768 (137
checks).

### 2026-10-21 — Grown-ups for real: the database holds 👪 Grown-ups, and the login's email and password change there (#167, #124d)
Every grown-up action needed only the family's sign-in, which a kid on the
shared device holds, so the 👪 PIN / password gate could be skipped with the
browser's developer tools. **Migration 0024** keeps a **15-minute** window per
sign-in (`grown_up_unlocks`, login + session id, no client can touch it):
the right grown-up PIN opens it (`try_parent_pin`; `check_parent_pin` too,
for old tabs), and so does a password typed just now — checked on
`passwordCheckClient`, whose fresh token (`amr` ≤ 60 s, as `delete-account`
reads it) calls `unlock_grown_ups(<the device's session>)`; a sign-in opens
*itself* only while the family is still being set up, so the first run
(consent → first player → their PIN) needs no gate and the minute after a
real sign-in can't be borrowed afterwards. Adding / removing / renaming
players (a trigger, plus the insert policy), `set_kid_pin`, `set_parent_pin`,
`record_consent`, the admin review and `delete_family` (the session the app
names — closing #167's last gap) refuse with SQLSTATE HQ001
`grown_ups_locked`; the kid's own play never does. The app opens it from the
gate (`GrownUpsGate`, shared), asks again over the page when it shuts
mid-action (`RelockDialog`: "👪 Grown-ups locked again", what was typed
kept, the step re-run — `asGrownUp`, `grownUpsStore`), and shuts it on
leaving 👪 Grown-ups or finishing setup (`useLockOnLeave`) and before a
grown-up's Sign out. **#124d:** 👪 Grown-ups → **Your sign-in** → Change
email… / Change password…, the current password typed again: the email via
`updateUser({ email })` on the device's own client (the page says which links
come, that the old email works until they're tapped, that devices stay signed
in; "already in use", bad addresses and rate limits in words), the password
via `updateUser({ password })` there too (this sign-in stays, Supabase signs
the others out; the emailed code when "Secure password change" wants one).
The privacy notice says how 👪 locks and that the login can be changed.
`delete-account` names the session and answers `grown_ups_locked`. Tests:
`grown_ups_gate.test.sql` + `close_account.test.sql` (12 loosenings of 0024,
each caught), the function against a stand-in 15/15 (the old one 5/15),
tests 2045 → 2112, lint, `tsc -b`, `build:web`, `deno check` of both functions, all
24 migrations + 17 SQL tests on Postgres 16, and the screens rendered at
320×568, 740×360 and 1024×768 (72/72). Logged: #168 (the auth service's own
user updates can't be held to the window).

### 2026-10-20 — Quiz review round 2: the next round asked for at its own level, held questions that never outlive the server's hold, a wait that starts when the slot is free, a results card that fits a sideways phone (#163)
`/saas-code-review` (2 medium, 1 low) and `/saas-ux-review` (4 low + 2 also
noted), all fixed; the lead's calls on the two open questions. **The next
round's questions are asked for as a round ends, at the level it's played
at:** a round its saved questions covered (`useBattleQuestions`' new
`covered`, through `useQuizRound`) has `finishRound` call `warmNextRound(…,
newLevel, …, { fresh: true })`, which makes the normal request (~1 fresh,
#30, `askFreshForNextRound`) there instead of the saved-only look-ahead —
it used to go out as the round started, at that round's level, so after a
climb of two about 2 of its 5 never fitted the next round and sat held and
unused. At the cap of 10 the oldest carried questions go (`carryOver` sorts
by hand-over time), never the new ones that fit. **A held question never
outlives the server's hold:** `invokeGenerate` notes when it handed each
question over, per kid (`noteHandedOver`, the request's send time), and a
carried question keeps that time — carrying it, putting it back unasked or
carrying it again no longer restarts its 45 minutes (it could be offered 75–84
min after hand-over, past the server's hour, so the kid could see it twice).
Tests for the per-kid routing and the catch path's carried questions (6 of
the reviewer's 14 mutations survived; all 14, and 19 more of this round's,
now fail a test). **The wait starts when the slot is free:** Next sets
`leaving`, cleared by `AnimatePresence`'s `onExitComplete`, and the calm
wait gets `waiting && !leaving` — a question that lands while the old card
slides out (~0.42 s) comes straight in, never behind a 0.17 s flash of "One
moment…" or held for its 0.4 s minimum. **At 8 s** the card says "This is
taking a while. You can go back and pick again." (no spinner, no "on its
way") over "Back to Topics", the status line says it once
(`LONG_WAIT_WORDS`), and the button goes the moment a question comes.
**Results on a short landscape phone** (`[@media(max-height:500px)]`): the
emoji (text-4xl) beside the title, the XP beside the score, tighter padding
and gaps — a perfect world round's card is 264 px at 740×360 (was 424, 140 px
past the screen), both buttons on screen; portrait and desktop unchanged.
The fact has its own mark, 🌟 (💡 is the recap's explanations). Five facts
reworded for a 7-year-old (no "asylum", the Cleopatra line, "slow
microwaves", factors, 99.965%) and the Sun's "99.8% of the mass" too, which
the new no-decimal-percentages test caught. **Also noted:** the wait block
and a question keep two lines' room (`min-h-[20.25rem]`, the h2
`min-h-[3.5rem]`), so a two-line question no longer grows the card 28 px; on
a short landscape phone it stays one line (the page already scrolls there,
and a second line would push every one-line question's last answer below
the fold). Starter-only rounds still show no XP line (the lead).
Stubbed app (BENCH_PORT 5781): the rest of a round released 150–600 ms
after Next → the wait never shows, no status line (375×667, 320×568,
740×360, 1024×768); 800 ms → shown from ~0.74 s for 0.42 s; at 8 s the new
words at three sizes, Tab reaches Back; results at 320×568, 375×667, 740×360
touch and 1024×768; a two-line question 424 px waiting and shown at 375×667
and 1024×768; a rich bank whose kid reads the results 25 s → every round
starts on the five asked at its level, none stranded, one call a round.
Tests 1844 → 1861.

### 2026-10-20 — The quiz starts at once review round 1: no skipped question, nothing wasted, a wait that never flashes, XP and a fun fact (#163)
`/saas-code-review` (1 high, 2 medium, 3 low) and `/saas-ux-review` (1
medium, 3 low), all fixed, with the lead's calls on the open questions.
**A double tap on Next skipped a question** (older than #163): the card
sliding out still ran its Next, so the recap, the Library and the bank /
starter split paired answers with the wrong questions. `advance(from)` moves
only from the question its Next belongs to, and a question sliding out is
`inert` (`RoundQuestion`, `useIsPresent`). The spare-starter timer fills
every empty slot up to the kid's (`withSpareStarters`), so the same tap into
a question not in yet can't leave "One moment…" up until the slow request
ends. **Nothing the server hands over is wasted** (the lead): what a round
can't use — in once every slot is taken, or not asked when the kid leaves,
or landing after — is carried to the kid's next round of the topic
(`carryOver` / `takeCarried`, `lib/questions.ts`: in memory, per kid, within
±2 levels, at most 10, dropped after `HELD_FOR_MS` = 45 min, inside the
server's hour-long hold) and goes first there (`quickQuestions`' `have`: with
3 it starts at once, asking only for the rest); the round-end look-ahead asks
only for what they leave missing (`warmNextRound`, `prefetchQuestions`'
`ask`). **~1 fresh question a round again on a rich bank** (#30, the lead):
when the saved questions cover the round, it asks in the background for the
next round's — the normal request for 5 a round made before #163, of which
the server writes about one fresh (`chooseFreshCount`) — carried over, one at
a time a topic, none when the next round is covered
(`askFreshForNextRound`); never waited for, and within the budget rules (one
call a round, ~1 fresh). A failing `quickQuestions` gives starters
(`useBattleQuestions`), and a wait past `LONG_WAIT_MS` (8 s; only a bug)
shows "Back to Topics". A look-ahead warmed longer ago than `HELD_FOR_MS`
isn't used (also noted). **The wait:** "One moment…" shows after 0.8 s for a
round's first question (0.25 s later) and then for at least 0.4 s
(`useCalmWait`), at a one-line question's height (`min-h-[18.5rem]`, 16rem on
a short landscape phone — measured, so the card never jumps); the status line
is in the page empty, speaks as the wait shows and says "Here's question N!"
when it ends. **Results:** "+N XP" under the score when the round paid some
(nothing for the opening rounds, #122), and one of the topic's fun facts
(`pickFact`). Dead code gone: `LoadingScreen`'s `topic` / `topBar`,
`ErrorScreen`'s `topBar` / `backLabel` / `onBack`, `Backdrop`'s bar. TC-49,
TC-1065, TC-1068, TC-1594 retired. Each guard's test fails without it (33
mutations). Stubbed app (BENCH_PORT 5741): saved questions in 100–700 ms → the
wait never shows, 900 ms → shown 0.4 s, no status line before; the card is
396 px waiting and with a question at 320×568 and 1024×768, 316 at 740×360
(no jump); two taps 120 ms apart move one question at all three sizes; 3
saved and the rest 45 s, double tap at question 3 → question 4, a starter at
~3.3 s; a thin bank (rest after 12 s) → round 1 two bank questions, round 2
starts on the three that came late, all five bank (+50 XP); a rich bank →
one background request a round, the look-ahead asks nothing. Tests 1816 →
1844.

### 2026-10-19 — The quiz starts at once (#163)
The Training Grounds quiz — the first screen a new kid sees — waited for its
whole round behind "Building your questions…" (`useGeneratedQuestions` →
`fetchQuestions`), 15–30 s on a thin bank. It now loads the battle's way
(#158): new `useQuizRound` (`features/quiz/`) runs `useBattleQuestions` for
the round's 5 — saved questions first (a saved-only request, at most
`QUICK_WAIT_MS`; fewer than 3 saved and starters fill the round), the rest
asked for while the kid answers, a late saved batch joining too — and takes
each question as the kid reaches it: the next bank question when one is in,
else a starter (`nextRoundQuestion`), never one the round asked. With nothing
new yet the card says "One moment… Your question is on its way!" (a status
line says it too; it fades in a beat late and leaves at once, so a quick
question never flashes it), and after `QUICK_WAIT_MS` comes a starter the
round hasn't had (`spareStarter`), so a round always finishes. The rules
stay: 5 a round, `PASS_THRESHOLD` / `ROUNDS_TO_UNLOCK`, the ramp on bank
answers only (`bankRamp`), no XP for starters or before the world opens, the
recap, 🚩, on screen = seen, the level pinned under the results. The
next-round look-ahead is saved-only now (`cacheOnly`, like a zone's
critters): nothing is written for a round the kid may never play, and the
round asks for the rest. `useGeneratedQuestions` and the quiz's error screens
are gone (neither could show since #153: the starters fill in); a loading bug
is logged in words (`console.warn(…, errorMessage(e))`), never shown. On the
stubbed app with a question server that answers saved-only requests at once
(3) and the rest after 15 s: the first question is in the page ~90 ms after
the tap at 320×568 and 1024×768 (main: "Building your questions…" for the
15 s); a kid at question 4 before the rest gets a starter after ~3 s; a
slower kid gets the late bank questions. Tests 1805 → 1816.
### 2026-10-20 — Safe to share review round 2: closing needs the password typed just now, one wait said once, focus that goes back where it was (#124b, #124c, #130 S6)
Fixes for `/saas-code-review` (4) and `/saas-ux-review` (6 + notes) of PR
#84. **Closing an account:** any password sign-in in the last 5 minutes —
a grown-up's ordinary sign-in, or opening 👪 Grown-ups by password — left a
token on the family device that could close the account without the
password typed. The close's re-sign-in and the gate's password check now run
on `passwordCheckClient` (`lib/supabase.ts`: memory only, never refreshed,
its own storage key, no other tab told) and are signed out after;
`delete-account` wants a password within **60 s**, refuses a token with no
`amr` (logged loudly) and needs `password` in a bare-string `amr` (the
minute after a real sign-in stays open — #167). After `login_not_deleted`,
"Keep my account" reloads the family (the privacy notice, not "＋ Add a
player" without consent). A sign-out the server can't hear no longer leaves
"Closing your account…" stuck (`signOutHere`: the stored session dropped,
then signed out again with no server needed; "Sign in again" too), and
`useAuthInit` follows the family store's `userId`. Close account has the
gate's reset link (`ResetLinkButton`). **PINs:** the lock sentence names no
time — the countdown is the one place it's said; a reopened pad asks before
it shows a remembered lock ("Checking…"); a tap on a locked key asks again;
locked keys leave the Tab order and are 44 px on a sideways phone (the lock
still fits at 740×360); the gate says "Use your password for now." while
its PIN rests. **Grown-ups list:** back from any page, focus and the page go
to the button that left it, once (no more jumping to "Close account…");
unlocking says "🔓 Sam can try their PIN again now." on the row and through
the area's live region; the gate, Change and Remove focus their titles.
**Plain errors:** "Sign in again" on KidForm, Remove and the grown-up PIN
form (`SignInAgainButton`); the kid pad's reads "Grown-ups: sign in again".
The CI lock test no longer prints the DB password. Tests 1891 → 1915 (each
fix mutation-checked); all 23 migrations + 16 SQL tests; the function against
a stand-in 36/36 (round 1's fails 7); rendered at 320×568, 740×360 and
1024×768 (28 checks each).

### 2026-10-19 — Safe to share review round 1: the database decides when a pad opens, closing never shares a sign-in limit, every refusal true (#124b, #124c, #130 S6)
Fixes for `/saas-code-review` (6) and `/saas-ux-review` (13 + notes) of PR
#84. **PINs:** a lock lifted elsewhere opened nowhere — the pad trusted the
deadline it remembered. Migration 0022 gains `pin_lock_status()` (the
caller's own locked kids and grown-up PIN, seconds left, nothing else);
`PinPad` asks it on opening, when the tab comes back and every 15 s, and
👪 Grown-ups marks a locked kid by name ("type their PIN again — the same one
is fine"). Remembered locks are per login and forgotten on sign-out. The kid
words are one kind word ("That's a lot of tries! Take a break for 5
minutes…", "2 more tries, then a short break."), the wait is said in words
(never "5:00"), the subtitle says "Taking a short break.", a reopened locked
pad is read out, a kid's offline line asks a grown-up, and a dead sign-in
offers "Sign in again" (the pads and `App`'s family error). On a phone on
its side the pad and its words sit side by side. A new CI test,
`pin_limits_lock.test.sql`, proves the row lock with two dblink sessions (it
fails without `for update`). **Closing an account:** the function no longer
signs in itself (one per-IP limit for every family): the app signs in again
from the grown-up's browser and the function checks the token's `amr`
password time (≤ 5 min). Every refusal is true in every path — a lost reply
says "we can't tell, safe to repeat", a login already gone counts as done,
"Nothing was deleted" only when it wasn't; after `login_not_deleted` the
device's copies go and Grown-ups lists no players. The page opens at the top
with its title focused, marks the box a refusal is about, uses sign-in's
labels, and "Keep my account" returns focus to its opener; the sign-in page
says "Your account and all its data are gone." in a focused banner. **Plain
errors:** remove-player and grown-up-PIN saves use `plainErrorOr`; 🚩 Report
and the world's question errors are read out; the 8-player line and the
draft notice's contact line fixed. Tests 1856 → 1891; all 23 migrations + 16
SQL tests (each new rule mutation-checked); the function against a stand-in
29/29 (the old one fails 20).

### 2026-10-19 — Safe to share: PIN guesses limited, a grown-up can close the account, every screen says it plainly (#124b, #124c, #130 S6)
A kid's 4-digit PIN could be guessed without limit by anyone holding the
family's session, and closing an account meant emailing us. **Migration
0022** counts wrong tries in the database: `try_kid_pin` / `try_parent_pin`
lock a PIN after 5 wrong in a row — 1 minute, then 5, then 15, then an hour
each time — refuse even the right PIN while locked (uncounted), and start
over on the right PIN, a new PIN (how a grown-up unlocks a kid) or a quiet
day; only the caller's own PINs count, and `check_kid_pin` /
`check_parent_pin` keep their signatures for old tabs. `PinPad` says it
plainly — "Too many tries — ask a grown-up, or wait 5 minutes." (the gate:
"Use your password instead") — counts the wait down, remembers it for the
tab (`lib/pinLock.ts`) and takes no digits meanwhile. **Close account**, at
the bottom of 👪 Grown-ups, lists exactly what goes, asks for the account's
password (never the grown-up PIN) and DELETE, and calls the new single-file
`delete-account` edge function (token and password checked there,
`delete_family` in migration 0023, then the login deleted with the service
role); the sign-in page then says "Your account and all its data are gone."
The family screens and `App`'s family error say plain words
(`plainErrorOr`, `familyLoadMessage`), and the world's question screens and
🚩 Report too (`questionErrorMessage`: "The Spire shudders! The riddles
didn't load. Tap "Try the stair again"."). Tests: `pin_limits.test.sql` and
`close_account.test.sql`, a 30-call burst counts exactly 5, the function
against a stand-in Supabase 20/20; 1805 → 1856 green, lint, `tsc -b`,
`deno check` of both functions, all 23 migrations + 15 SQL tests on
Postgres 16. Logged: #167 (the grown-ups gate is a UI lock only).
### 2026-10-20 — Back to the Library: a returning kid is sent to Librarian Sage (#166)
The owner: now that typing is in, whenever someone logs back in a message
should tell them Sage has something for them at the Library. `WorldScreen`
shows "📚 Librarian Sage has something for you at the Library in Lumina
Village — go and see him!" as a world toast (read out) a beat after the
world opens, once a session per kid (`battleStore.libraryNudgeSaid`, reset by

waits out a story, an overlay or a toast already up (retrying for up to

means it's said again next time), plays only once the intro has been seen,
and never on a glass keyboard. Tests: `WorldScreen.nudge.test` (+5,

typing` 48/48 on the changed screen (the toast in the HUD shots).

### 2026-10-19 — Touch typing I: Librarian Sage's typing lessons (#165)
The owner and their brother: kids don't type any more, so the game teaches
it. First of three: the lessons.
- **In the Library** (📚 Library → ⌨️ Typing lessons, `features/typing/`):
  13 lessons in the classic order — F J, D K, S L, A ;, E I, R U, T O, G H,
  the corners, V N M B, C X Z , ., capitals with the other hand's Shift,
  then true-fact sentences — each open once the one before is cleared
  (`typing:<id>` flags), 30 XP the first time, 1–3 stars for accuracy, the
  ✍️ Scribe's Quill badge for all 13. Librarian Sage's lines name them.
- **The keyboard and the hands** (`KeyboardDiagram`): drawn from the
  physical key map (`content/typing.ts`, the standard finger for every
  key); the key to press glows gold, the finger for it lights on the hand.
- **The home-row hold** (the owner's safeguard): the other hand must hold
  its bump key — J for a left-hand letter, F for a right-hand one — or the
  other Shift for a capital. A letter without it is a nudge, never a miss;
  Caps Lock gets its own. Switched per player (`typingAnchor`, 📜 Menu →
  ⌨️ Typing and the lessons list); under 8 the list suggests going slow.
- **A real keyboard first:** `keyboardStore` / `watchKeyboard`; the lessons'
  gate explains on a phone. Keys never leave the browser.
- Tests: +47 (`content/typing.test`, `lib/typing.test`, `keyboardStore.test`,
  `TypingLessons.test`, `save.test`); lint + tsc clean. Follow-ups #165.

### 2026-10-19 — #159 review round 2: saving is never a fight, the boat a walk away, Escape that always lands, sign-in words for every case
Second `/saas-code-review` + `/saas-ux-review` pass over the whole PR (16
findings: 0 critical, 0 high, 4 medium), with the round-1 follow-ups; the
owner said fix all.
- **World:** from every spawn and arrival there's a walk to each save crystal
  outside every critter's reach, and none wanders up beside one (two new
  `zones.test` rules; 15 critters re-homed — Verdara's field four now roam
  its top); roaming villagers are kept out of reach too. A Biscuit moored on
  the hero's own coast a walk away (`boatWalkable`) gets "Walk … to the
  Biscuit (⛵ on your map) and sail east" and the 🚩 on the ⛵; Old Marlow
  only for a boat on another map or in open water; the guides' "why" no
  longer says where she waits. A layer closing over another whose button
  went away hands focus to the layer below (`refocusTopLayer`); with none
  left focus goes to the world's stage (`data-keys-home`), so Space stays the
  world's. Escape on "Leave the Spire?" hands the keys back to the climb,
  "⛵ Row her home" moves focus to Bye!, the Library pays its XP on the right
  answer (Leave / Escape never loses it), PathQuestion's Later / Walk away
  take Escape, the seals are heard as "Seals: 1 of 3", the Fiend-gate hints
  speak from "where you came in". The fog's skip button sits beside its line
  on a keyboard computer, and on a phone it takes the help line's room, so
  the pad never jumps.
- **Battle and quiz:** a win or loss shuts the banners until the next fight
  (a late "So quick!" never lands on the result); every damage number has a
  dark rim; a sideways phone's quiz scrolls "Next Question" and its 💡 line
  into view.
- **Sign-in:** a proxy's HTML error page or a 5xx is "having trouble right
  now", a dead sign-in "Your sign-in ran out — please sign in again", an
  email link that ran out is said on the sign-in page (`linkError.ts`), an
  unmapped error says what didn't happen on that screen, "check spam" only
  after a resend, a 0 s wait is 1 s. "Send reset link" and the gate's "Forgot
  it?" count a refusal down like the resend (`useRetryWait`); buttons wait
  with `aria-disabled`; a dead reset link makes "Back to sign in" the main
  button; the gate's links are 44 px; repeated messages are read again
  (`useLiveMessage`).
- **Bench:** `bench … hud` also plays sideways phones by touch (740×360 and
  568×320, walking and flying with Calm), and its Spire check follows the new
  Escape rule (8/8).
Tests 1761 → 1805; `bench … battle` 5/5.

### 2026-10-19 — #159 review round 1: Escape that never goes dead, Old Marlow everywhere, sideways phones fit, errors that name the screen
Two `/saas-code-review` and two `/saas-ux-review` passes over the PR (40
findings: 0 critical, 0 high); the owner said fix all.
- **World:** `ModalLayer` hears Escape on the page while it's the top layer
  and pulls focus back in when the focused button goes away or turns off
  (Rest, a price you can't pay, ✨ Learn, a Library answer, ⛵ Row her home);
  closed by Escape, the world gets the keys back. A dialogue's first line is
  its description (`describedBy`), so one-line talks are heard. With the
  Biscuit away from the dock (another map, or down the coast — `atDock`),
  Grandmother Wick, Scout Tamsin and the signposts also start with Old Marlow
  (`wayfindingLines`' `boat`), the way goes on to the dock, and the 🚩 marks
  his shore (`seaWayStart`, one source for words and flag); sailing, the words
  say "Sail east to the Silver Shallows!". A fog reveal shows a 44 px "⏩ Tap
  to skip" button under the map (the 3 px canvas pill is gone). On a sideways
  phone the HUD spans the page and the help line steps aside, so the map is
  never cut. The Woods' Snapjaw is back in the open, Verdara's two critters
  are off the hidden glade's mouth, no level tag sits under a roof (new
  `zones.test` / `sleepMark.test` rules, gates open too); the ⛵ is never
  nudged off the menu map; a hot reload loads new sprite sheets.
- **Sign-in:** errors say what the server meant (a stricter password rule's
  length or missing kinds, a leaked password, the hourly email cap, a 5xx
  "having trouble right now", a mail failure) and name the screen's own
  controls (`authErrorMessage(err, { where })`); only a real expired link
  says so. "📨 Send the link again" keeps focus (`aria-disabled`), reads its
  wait in words and counts down a wait a refusal named; the inbox says what
  to do if you already had an account; the gate and reset page announce
  their messages; "Details for grown-ups" looks like it opens.
- **Battle and quiz:** 🏃 Flee is last in the tab order; swap / Pair Attack
  banners cut in, a win clears the rest, the cleanup resets the queue, each
  banner is keyed; a super-effective hit floats a big gold "-42!!". The
  quiz keeps its top bar while loading or failing, "Switch player" from
  400 px, a short landscape phone tightens the card. The icon is centred at
  80%, the home-screen one opaque on the theme colour. Tooling: the edge
  imports test catches side-effect imports, `favicon.test` reads by URL, the

### 2026-10-17 — Battles start at once (#158)
Bumping a critter showed "… approaches" for 15–20 s: `BattleArena` waited for
all 9 questions, and the server asked Claude for whatever the bank lacked
before answering. New `useBattleQuestions` (replacing `useGeneratedQuestions`
in the battle) starts on saved questions — `quickQuestions` takes the zone's
saved-only look-ahead, or makes a saved-only request (never Claude, at most
`QUICK_WAIT_MS` = 3 s), and needs only `BATTLE_START_COUNT` = 3 (starters fill
in) — then `moreQuestions` asks for the rest as before and the pool grows
when they arrive (the battle goes round what it has meanwhile). Bench with a
15 s question server: on screen in 25 ms. Tests: questions.test (+6),
useBattleQuestions.test (new, 2); 1577 green.

### 2026-10-16 — Ages 6 to 18, one-word answers for young readers (#156)
The owner widened the audience from about 7–11 to **6–18**. The question
writer's prompt (`generate-questions/index.ts` `SYSTEM_PROMPT`) now says
"aged 6 to 18", adds what children know at 6, 13, 15 and 17+, and a **YOUNG
READERS** block: at 6 or under, a question of about 6 simple words and every
option one 3–4 letter word or a small number (the shortest everyday word when a
topic has none). The blind check (`CHECK_PROMPT`, `_shared/questionScreen.ts`
and its inline copy) fails a young reader's question that breaks that. Reuse:
a kid of `YOUNG_READER_AGE` (6) or under never gets a question written for an
older child (`p_max_age` = their age), only their own or younger. The app caps
`playerAge` at `MAX_PLAYER_AGE` (18), as the function already clamped; anyone
older plays as 18. Public pages, the game's meta description and the README say
6–18. Deploy: redeploy `generate-questions` (no migration).
**Teen start level (the owner's call):** `ageToStartLevel` was `age − 5`, so
15+ started every topic at 10 — the hardest *for their age*, since levels are
relative to age. It now stops at `MAX_START_LEVEL` (6, where an 11-year-old
started): 6 → 1 … 11 and older → 6. Only topics without a saved level use it;
kids already playing keep theirs.

### 2026-10-16 — The public pages go live at `/`, the game moves to `/play/` (#146i)
The owner's go-ahead on #146 (b), in a draft PR.
- **`npm run build:web`** (`scripts/build-web.mjs`): builds the game as
  before (base `/`), moves its `index.html` to `dist/play/`, then builds
  `site/` into `dist/` beside it (its pages, its bundle in `assets/`, the
  game's `public/` files at the root, where both the pages and the game ask
  for them). It fails if any of the three pages is missing.
- **Vercel** (`vercel.json`): `buildCommand` `npm run build:web`,
  `outputDirectory` `dist`, and `/play` → `/play/`. **CI** runs `build:web`
  in place of `build`, so it checks what's deployed.
- **Links meant for the game that land on `/`** — Supabase's emails go to the
  project's Site URL, review alerts open `?review` — are passed on to
  `/play/` with their address by a script at the top of `site/index.html`
  (tokens or an error in the hash; `?code`, `?token_hash`, `?error`,
  `?review`). The pages' own anchors (`#contact`) stay. So nothing breaks
  before the owner adds `/play/` to Supabase's Redirect URLs.
- No game code changes: the game's paths are all absolute, its sign-up and
  reset links use the page's own address (`/play/`), and `?signup` already
  pointed there.
- **Checked** (the build served by a plain static server, Supabase stubbed,
  headless Chromium): `/` shows the kids' page with every sprite and no
  errors; Play free opens Sign in at `/play/`; the grown-ups sign-up button
  opens the sign-up form (and drops `?signup`); `/play` opens the game at
  `/play/` with no failed requests; the game's sprites, tiles, backdrops,
  music and favicon load from the root; a reset link, an expired-link error,
  `?code` and `?review` at `/` land on `/play/` with the same address; `#contact`,
  `/grown-ups.html#contact` and `?utm_source` stay. Lint, tests and build
  clean.
- ⚠️ Owner: add `https://hazelapp-pi.vercel.app/play/` to Supabase → Authentication →
  URL Configuration → Redirect URLs (reset links then go straight to the
  game). Families' bookmarks of the old address now open the public page,
  one tap from the game; saves and sign-ins are untouched (same site).

### 2026-10-16 — Make the Spire fairer: gentler levels, an easier climb after each fall (Act I launch review B4, #150)
The launch review found a kid getting 75–80% right cleared the Spire only a
quarter to half of the time: its questions were 2–6 levels above the kid's
own (`levelBonus` 1–5, plus one more in `SpireOverlay.loadFloor`), four
candles had to last 19 questions, and a loss changed nothing.
- **Gentler levels:** the floors ask 0, 1, 2, 2 and 3 levels above the kid
  (Umbra's floor highest); the extra +1 is gone. One rule,
  `spireQuestionLevel` (kept to 1–10), sets the floors and Umbra's challenge.
- **Easier after a fall:** losing a climb sets a save flag (`spire-falls-1`,
  then `spire-falls-2`; no save field, no version bump). The next climb has
  one more candle and asks one level lower, up to two falls (`spireFalls`,
  `spireCandles`); clearing the Spire forgets them. Back after a fall, one
  line follows the intro: "The Spire remembers you tried. One more candle —
  and the riddles are a little easier." `spireStore.reset(lives)` starts a
  climb with its candles (`lives` and `maxLives`; the canvas light is sized
  by the candles left alone, so it starts wider after a fall). Chosen
  over restarting from the highest floor: the climb's progress is never
  saved, and fresh candles for each floor would make every floor easy while
  Umbra's questions stayed as hard.
- **Elder Lumen's Spire tip** said to rest at the inn, but HP isn't used in
  the Spire. Now: "🪶 Hint Feathers work in the Spire too — each one hides two
  wrong answers. Peddler Plus sells them in Numbria. Bring a few!"
- **Odds** (accuracy held fixed, so only the candles help): at 75% right a
  climb cleared 26% of the time; now the first climb is the same, the second
  47%, the third 67%. At 80%: 46%, then 67% and 84%. If each level easier
  adds 3 points of accuracy, the first climb at 75% clears 56%.
- **Tests:** +10 (spire, spireStore, SpireOverlay — the real climb lost and
  won; wayfinding's tip changed); 1379 green, lint and build clean. In
  headless Chromium with Supabase stubbed: a 10-year-old's first floor asks
  level 5 (was 7); after one fall, level 4, 5 candles and the line; after two,
  level 3 and 6 candles, which fit a 320 px phone.

### 2026-10-16 — B9 review round 2: a removed kid's requests go at once, a crash gives back its hold (#152)
A fresh `/saas-code-review` of PR #67 (1 medium, 2 low), all fixed:
- **A removed kid's requests weren't deleted "straight away" (medium):**
  removing a kid unlinked every request row they'd ever made (its time, its
  count, the grown-up's id), and only the next question request *anywhere*
  deleted the ones over a day old — hours later in a quiet spell — while the
  day's rows kept every request's time, spent or not (the draft notice says
  only a count is kept). That next request also deleted the whole history
  inside its quota check, under its family's lock. Now a `before delete`
  trigger on `profiles` (`profiles_forget_requests`, security definer like
  0016's) deletes at once the kid's rows no budget needs — over a day old, or
  nothing spent; today's spend stays, unlinked, and goes once a day old.
- **A crash kept the hold for the day (low):** an error thrown between the
  quota check and the usage write reached the catch-all with the whole batch
  still held — up to 15 fresh questions charged to the kid, the family and
  the project for 24 h for a call that served nothing (a crash loop could
  spend the project's day). The catch-all now writes what Claude made before
  the error (`made`) in the hold's place — never less, so never an unlimited
  bill; without 0021 (nothing held) it now records that spend too, which
  was left at 0.
- **TEST-CASES types (low):** "I" (#133's rows use it too) is in the legend
  as integration; the two manual psql runs (TC-1374, TC-1377) are M; TC-1331
  names the six-argument function.
- **Tests:** `family_budget.test.sql` (the grown-up removes a kid through
  `authenticated`: the 40-day-old row and the ones that spent nothing go at
  once, today's 4 stay, a brother's rows are untouched — fails without the
  trigger, without `security definer` and without the nothing-spent rule);
  `family.test.ts` (+1); the function against a stand-in (7 checks; 3 fail on
  round 1's version; round 1's 9 still pass). 1372 tests green, lint + build
  clean, all 9 SQL tests, the migrations in order and the bundle twice pass
  on Postgres 16, `deno check` clean.
- Open: a removed kid's spend from today goes at the first question request
  after it's a day old (there's no scheduled job), so in a quiet project it

### 2026-10-16 — A question budget per family (#152)
Act I launch review B9, the code that was left. Since #124 every kid had their
own daily budget of fresh (Claude-written) questions, and removing a kid
removed their request log with them: a grown-up could add a kid, spend, remove
them and repeat without end, and each of up to 8 kids added a full budget.
- **Migration 0021** (`0021_family_question_budget.sql`): a request log row
  outlives its kid (`profile_id` → null on delete, was cascade), and records
  the grown-up (`question_requests.parent_id`, null when they go; filled in
  for rows already logged). `begin_question_request` takes a fifth argument,
  `p_fresh_per_family` (default null = no family cap, so a four-argument call
  still works): the fresh allowance is the least of the kid's, the family's
  and the project's; the advisory lock is per family now, so brothers and
  sisters calling at once can't slip under the cap together.
- **The function** sends the cap (`FRESH_PER_FAMILY_PER_DAY`, default 400 —
  two kids' worth; a secret overrides it). Before 0021 is applied the five-
  argument call doesn't exist, so it checks per kid as before and logs
  "FAMILY BUDGET UNAVAILABLE"; a quota check that fails both ways still
  serves the cache only.
- **Tests:** `supabase/ci/family_budget.test.sql` (the family shares its cap,
  another family is untouched, no cap without the argument, a removed kid's
  spend stays and remove-and-re-add doesn't reset the day, the project still
  counts it, a day rolls it off, a removed grown-up's id goes, grants) — it
  fails with 0014's cascade back; `quota.test.sql` checks the new signature,
  `pins.test.sql` says a removed kid's requests are unlinked. All 9 SQL tests,
  the migrations in order and the bundle twice pass on Postgres 16; `deno
  check` clean; the function run locally against a stand-in: the cap is sent,
  without 0021 it falls back per kid, and a failed check never calls Claude.
- ⚠️ Deploy: apply 0021 (or re-run `apply_all_migrations.sql`), then
  `supabase functions deploy generate-questions`. Either order works.
  Follow-ups #152.

### 2026-10-16 — S1 review round 1: no expired token on hide, a failed hide save tried again (#151)
`/saas-code-review` of the S1 PR (1 medium; the trigger, the load / flush

save on hide sent the token `authStore` held even after it expired (the
server refuses it with a 401), and a failed hide push was never retried while
the page stayed open, because hiding had cancelled the debounce. Now
`upsertSaveKeepalive` gives null for a token that has expired or will within
30 s (`KEEPALIVE_TOKEN_MARGIN_MS`), so the normal request (which refreshes
it) goes instead, and a hide push that fails without a refusal re-arms the

tests fail without them). 1401 green, lint + build clean; the stub, all 17
migrations and the bundle applied twice on Postgres 16, all 9 SQL tests pass.

### 2026-10-16 — Saves keep the newest progress: revisions, a server check, save on hide (launch checklist S1, #151)
The launch review's S1: the server's copy always won on load (progress kept
on the device was overwritten), a closing tab didn't save (2 s debounce),
and two devices or tabs overwrote each other silently.
- **A revision on every save:** `SaveData.revision`, one more on every change
  (`saveStore.update`; returning the save unchanged makes none). Additive —
  `normalizeSave` gives a save without one (or a junk one) 0, `revisionOf`
  reads it as the server does; no `SAVE_VERSION` bump (an old app is stopped
  by the server rule below; an old tab that loads a revisioned save can only
  lose its own unsaved moves).
- **Load keeps the newer copy:** the local copy and the server's are compared;
  the higher revision wins, a newer local copy is pushed, a tie (or neither
  with a revision) goes to the server's as before. A copy from a newer version
  on *either* side → `'outdated'`, neither touched. The server's copy is only
  written back when it needs it (an older shape, or no revision yet), as a new
  revision. A second kid's load drops the first kid's late answer.
- **The server refuses an older copy:** migration `0020_save_revisions.sql`
  redefines 0011's trigger function: a stored revision may only be replaced
  by a higher one or the identical save (a retried upload); a save without a
  revision (an app from before this) can't replace one, and a row without a
  revision takes anything until the new app saves it once. Same error,
  `save_version_conflict`, so old tabs show their "refresh" screen; detail
  `save_revision_conflict`. Hand edits in SQL now need the revision raised.
- **Told to reload, kindly:** a refused tab goes `'outdated'` with
  `outdatedReason` `'elsewhere'` and `OutdatedScreen` (StatusScreens) says
  "🧭 Your adventure moved on! — You played on in another tab or on another
  device. Refresh the page to carry on from your newest save." (the update
  screen keeps its copy for `'update'`). Its local copy is marked revision 0
  so the refresh can't pick it over the server's. A tab also stops when
  another tab on this browser saves a newer copy (`storage`) or the server
  has one when the tab comes back into view (`checkForNewer`).
- **Save on hide:** `watchPageForSaves` (called once in `App`): on
  `visibilitychange` → hidden and `pagehide`, `flushOnHide` pushes what the
  server hasn't got at once, as a `keepalive` fetch built synchronously with
  the token `authStore` holds (`lib/saveKeepalive.ts` — supabase-js awaits an
  auth lock first, which a closing page may never finish); over 60 KiB or with
  no session it falls back to a normal upsert. Best effort on a closing page
  (CORS preflight, Firefox < 133); the local copy always has it.
- `flush` sends one push at a time and skips a copy the server already has
  (`syncedRevision`); a refusal of an older push of ours, overtaken by a newer
  one, is ignored.
- Between two copies that both moved on, the one with more changes wins — the
  other is told to reload (ISSUES #151 (a): a per-push base check would make
  it strict).
- Tests +29: saveStore.test (which copy wins, two devices, the refused copy,
  overlapping pushes, hide / pagehide, back in view, another tab), save.test
  (revision, `revisionOf`), saveKeepalive.test, StatusScreens.test; the fake
  server keeps a row per kid and applies 0020's rules. 1398 green, lint +
  tsc + build clean; every saveStore rule checked by breaking it (14
  mutations, all caught). SQL: `supabase/ci/save_revisions.test.sql` (fails
  without 0020); the stub + all 17 migrations and the bundle applied twice on
  Postgres 16, all 9 SQL tests pass. Bundle regenerated (17 migrations).
- ⚠️ Deploy: apply `0020_save_revisions.sql` (or re-run
  `apply_all_migrations.sql`) **before** shipping this build.

  Chromium at 375 and 1024 px: no sideways scroll, no errors.

### 2026-10-16 — Checked once, and again on a report: Haiku, then Opus (#147)
The owner's follow-up on PR #71: existing questions checked once, new ones on
generation, and a report triggering a check by Haiku and then a stronger model.
- **Once:** migration 0018 adds `checked_version` / `checked_at`; new questions
  are saved with `RULES_VERSION` (only when the checks ran; without 0018 the
  save retries without it), and the recheck script skips checked questions and
  marks what passes.
- **A report checks it again:** the app asks `generate-questions` with
  `action: 'check-report'` after a report (not awaited); `claim_report_check`
  lets each report start one check, 20 a family a day; `reportReview` runs
  Claude Haiku, then Claude Opus 5.5 if Haiku finds nothing (higher effort, the
  server-side refusal fallback). A problem or a check that can't run holds the
  question back; a check never puts one back. `--reported` sweeps the rest.
- **Shared:** `runChecks` and `reportReview` in the screening block, over a
  `CallModel` each caller builds (`modelCaller`); the sign-in and kid checks
  are one helper (`identifyKid`) for both actions.
- ⚠️ Deploy: apply 0017 and 0018, deploy the function, then the recheck.
- **Tests:** questionScreen.test (+ runChecks, reportReview — escalation, holds,
  errors; mutations caught), questions.test (+3), `question_checks.test.sql`
  (each claim rule fails with its line removed); the harness 84/84. 1391 green;
  lint, build, `deno check` clean; ten SQL tests and the bundle twice on Postgres 16.

### 2026-10-16 — Safer questions: Haiku 5.5, an explanation check, an age window, a bank recheck (#147)
The question prompt rewrite and the seven fixes from its `/saas-code-review`.
- **Claude Haiku 5.5** writes and checks questions (was Haiku 4.5: newer and
  about a tenth of the price per token). It thinks by default and thinking
  counts toward `max_tokens`, so the writer gets 16,000 (was 4,096) and the
  checks 8,000 / 4,000 (the check had 1,024 — it would have been cut off and
  every fresh question dropped); effort is set per call; timeout 30 s (was 20).
- **New prompts:** ten numbered safety rules for every part of a question,
  wrong options and explanation included (privacy, nothing a child could copy,
  stereotypes, disputed or changing facts, facts true in one country only);
  the writer pitches the level to the child's age with examples by age and
  checks its own work; the blind check fails a question two options could
  answer; both checks ignore instructions inside the questions.
- **An explanation check** (the blind check never saw the explanation), side
  by side with the blind check; both must pass. Their prompts are the same for
  every child, so all three prompts are cached now.
- **Nothing points at an option by its place** (`screenQuestion`): "all of
  the above", "the answer is B", lettered options — the options get shuffled.
- **History and geography** steer away from conquest, slavery, disputed
  borders and country counts.
- **Reuse keeps to the age** (migration 0017): within a year either way, an
  older child's question only at or below the kid's level; without 0017 the
  function retries the old picker.
- **The bank recheck** (`supabase/scripts/recheck-questions.ts`): the same
  checks over the saved bank, failures to `review` with a `review_note`, which
  the queue now lists even with no reports.
- ⚠️ Deploy: apply 0017, run the recheck (dry run, then `--apply`), then
  `supabase functions deploy generate-questions`.
- **Tests:** questionScreen.test (rewritten, 37), `question_age_window.test.sql`
  (each rule fails with its line removed); a local harness ran the function and
  the script against stand-in Supabase and Claude (52/52). 1383 green; lint,
  build, `deno check` (both) clean; migrations and the bundle twice on
  Postgres 16 with all nine SQL tests.

(a title screen with Kira and Ember walking, a playable demo battle, heroes,

and what they rest on are listed in `site/README.md`. Not live: the privacy
notice is still a draft and the game would move to `/play/` (#146).

### 2026-10-12 — #134 code review round 1: a bounded window, a real 20-id cap, ages kept on errors
A fresh-context `/saas-code-review` of PR #55 (1 medium, 1 low-medium, 2 low;
no security hole between families). All fixed:
- **The picker read a kid's whole history (medium):** the seen window
  grouped every shown view they'd ever had, so a call grew with play —
  measured 440–560 ms at 75k shown views. It now reads only their latest
  `10 × p_seen_limit` shown views off the `(profile_id, seen_at desc)`
  index: ~95 ms with 150k views and a 40k-row band (most of it the band's
  sort).
- **A nested array slipped past the 20-id cap (low-medium):** `p_ids[1:20]`
  cut only the first dimension (a 25×2 array wrote 40 rows); now
  `unnest(p_ids) limit 20`. A question already marked shown for that kid in
  the last half hour isn't written again.
- **Any picker error dropped ages (low):** only "no such function"
  (`PGRST202` / `42883`, `PICKER_MISSING`) means 0015 isn't applied; a
  timeout still stores ages and holds, so no question escapes the age rule.
- **The app never re-sent an id in a long-lived tab (low):** `markSeen`
  re-sends a question shown again after `SEEN_RESEND_MS` (30 min), and a
  failed send is retried the next time the question shows.
- **The window is 100 per topic** (round 1 made it 200): the same as before
  for a kid playing one topic, so Claude spend per kid doesn't rise.
- Tests: question_reuse.test.sql (+ the scan cap, a 25×2 array, a repeat
  within half an hour — each fails with its fix reverted), seen.test (+2).
  1123 green, lint + build clean, `deno check` clean.

### 2026-10-12 — Fairer question reuse: the whole bank, the right age, seen = shown (#134)
A review of how questions are picked and reused (with the owner) found three
problems; this is batch 1 of the fixes, as the owner chose them.
- **The whole bank:** the edge function read 200 rows of a ±2 level band
  with no ORDER BY, so Postgres returned the same oldest 200 on every call
  (measured: 15,037 rows in a band, the same 200 every time, all from the
  oldest ~1,700) — past 200 a band's new questions were never reused. New
  `pick_cached_questions` (migration 0015) samples the whole band, weighted
  random leaning to less-asked (1/sqrt(1 + `times_asked`) — the owner's
  pick over purely random), unseen first, flagged never.
- **The right age:** new questions store the age they were written for
  (`questions.age`); reuse skips ones written for a child more than a year
  older (`AGE_SLACK`). Questions from before have no age and stay reusable
  (the owner's call).
- **Seen = shown:** prefetches (every enemy in a zone, each battle's 4 spell
  questions, the next quiz round) counted as seen though never shown, and the
  seen window was the last 100 of any topic. Now the app marks a question
  seen when it's on screen (`lib/seen.ts` `markSeen` — batched every 1.5 s,
  at most 20 a call, each id once a session per kid — called from
  `QuestionCard` and `QuizRound`; `mark_questions_seen` writes only for the
  signed-in grown-up's own kid), the app sends `reportsSeen: true`, and the
  function's deliveries are only held back for an hour
  (`question_views.shown = false`, `RESERVE_MINUTES`). The window is the
  last 200 distinct questions shown in that topic.
- **Deploy order is free:** without 0015 the function falls back to the old
  lookup (`legacyCachePool`, logged "QUESTION PICKER FAILED") and stores no
  age; an app without `reportsSeen` keeps "delivered = seen".
- ⚠️ Deploy: apply `0015_fair_question_reuse.sql` (or re-run
  `apply_all_migrations.sql`), then `supabase functions deploy
  generate-questions`.
- Tests: `supabase/ci/question_reuse.test.sql` (band, age, flags, the
  per-topic distinct window, the hold, per kid, whole-band sampling, the lean,
  `mark_questions_seen`'s ownership / cap, grants) — checked to fail with no
  random order, plain random, no ownership check and no topic filter;
  `seen.test` (+6), QuestionCard.test (+1), QuizRound.test (+1),
  questions.test. 1121 green, lint + build clean, `deno check` clean; the
  migrations in order and the bundle twice on Postgres 16, all seven SQL
  tests pass. Batch 2 and follow-ups: ISSUES #134.
### 2026-10-12 — Screen AI questions for kids, and act on reports (#133)
Act I launch review B10 (the code part), built on #131's lockdown.
- **Rules in the prompt:** `CONTENT_RULES` joins the writer's system prompt;
  the history line drops "world history" and "important people" (never wars,
  battles, disasters or religion) and creativity is facts only
  (`_shared/topics.ts` and the inline copy).
- **A screen on every question** (`screenQuestion`): four different non-empty
  options, a right answer, length caps (220 / 80 / 280 characters), no links,
  emails or phone numbers, no word from a short blocked list (kept narrow:
  "killer whale", "shell", "grape", "dam" pass). Saved questions go through it
  too, so rows cached before the rules are screened as they're read.
- **The blind second check** (`checkFresh`): one more Haiku call gets the
  fresh questions without their answers, picks an answer for each and says
  whether it keeps the rules (`CHECK_SCHEMA`); only those it agrees with are
  served (`keepChecked`). The function asks for a third more than it needs to
  cover drops. If the check fails or its reply doesn't line up, no fresh
  questions are served — the cache fills the batch, as for a failed
  generation. About a third more Claude cost per fresh batch; secret
  `QUESTION_SECOND_CHECK=off` turns it off.
- **Reports:** 🚩 Report this question now sits on every question screen
  (`QuestionCard` — battles, gates, chests, shrines, the Library, the Spire —
  as well as the quiz), with **"Not OK for kids"** as the first reason and
  bigger tap targets. A report hides the question from the reporting family
  (all their kids); migration **0016** adds `questions.status`
  (`active` / `review` / `retired` / `approved`, `reviewed_at`) and a trigger:
  "not OK for kids" sends an active question to review at once, two families
  an approved one, three families' reports of any kind an active one. Only
  active and approved questions are served, approved first.
  `question_review_queue` (a view) and `review_question()` are the owner's
  (service role / SQL editor; no app role can read or call them). Before,
  one report from any family hid a question from everyone for good.
- Tests: `questionScreen.test.ts` (drift, the screen, `keepChecked`, the
  wiring), FlagButton / QuestionCard (+2), `supabase/ci/question_review.test.sql`
  (fails without the trigger). 1144 green, lint + build + `deno check` clean;
  the SQL tests, migrations in order and the bundle twice pass on Postgres 16;
  the function run locally against a stand-in Supabase + Claude: 42/42 checks
  (14 new for B10).
- ⚠️ Deploy: apply `0016_question_review.sql` **before** deploying the
  function (it reads `questions.status`), then
  `supabase functions deploy generate-questions`. Follow-ups #133.

### 2026-10-12 — Lock down the question service: fixed prompt text, the kid's id required, Claude failures fall back to the cache (#131)
Act I launch review B8, B9 (code part) and B5 (server part), all in
`generate-questions/index.ts`, plus the app sending flavour keys.
- **B8, nothing a caller types reaches the prompt:** the free-text `context`
  (up to 300 characters, cached for every child) is gone. The app sends a
  `flavor` key (`FlavorId`: battle, spell, gate, chest, shrine, spire) and the
  function looks up its own line (`QUESTION_FLAVORS`, inline; canonical in
  `_shared/flavors.ts`, drift-tested by `questionFlavors.test.ts`; an unknown
  key, or `constructor`, means no flavour). The kid's age comes from their
  birth date in `profiles` (`ageFrom`, clamped 3–18), not the request (which
  allowed 3–100). The level is rounded to a whole number. Six call sites send
  keys now (Spire, shrine trial, gate / chest, spell pool, speed boost).
- **B9, no unlimited fresh questions:** a request without `profileId` gets a
  400 ("Please refresh the page") instead of falling back to the login (which
  has no budget, so the quota check errored and the call went through with no
  limit); a failed kid lookup is a 503, not a fallback; and a failed quota
  check now fails **closed** — cache only, no Claude call.
- **B5, Claude failing doesn't stop play:** the Anthropic client times out at
  20 s with one retry (was the SDK's 10 min and two); `stop_reason` other than
  `end_turn` (cut off at `max_tokens`, refused) throws before `JSON.parse`; the
  Claude call has its own try/catch, and whatever it didn't deliver is topped up
  from the cache (unseen, then seen). Nothing left → 503 "The question wizards
  are busy right now — try again in a minute!" (budget spent → 429 "…come back
  tomorrow"). Raw Anthropic and parse errors are logged (typed: connection,
  API, other), never returned.
- **Checked:** `deno check` (CI's command) clean; the function run locally in
  Deno against a stand-in Supabase and Claude API — 28 checks pass (signed-out,
  no kid, another family's kid, lookup failure, the prompt's age / flavour /
  no free text, rounding, quota failure with and without a cache, budget, rate
  limit, Claude 529 with one retry, a cut-off reply, a refusal, a short batch).
  `main`'s version fails 17 of them. Tests: `questionFlavors.test` (+3),
  `questions.test` (+1). 1119 green, lint + build clean.
- ⚠️ Deploy: `supabase functions deploy generate-questions` **before** the app
  (the new app sends `flavor`; the old function ignores it, so either order
  works, but only the new function closes the holes).

### 2026-10-12 — Act I launch review: quick fixes B3, B2, S14, S6, B7 (#130)
The first quick fixes from the Act I launch review (the checklist's item
codes in brackets). **B3:** the quiz's "What you missed" recap showed the
wrong question — saving the new skill level re-keys `useGeneratedQuestions`,
which fetched the next batch under the results screen (and could show its
spinner instead). `QuizRound` now holds the finished round (`FinishedRound`:
questions, answers, picks) and renders `RoundResults` from it, before the
loading check. **B2:** tablets and sideways phones couldn't walk — the
`TouchPad` hid at `sm`. Two Tailwind screens (`tailwind.config.js`):
`touch` = `(pointer: coarse)` (the main pointer, like `WorldCanvas`'s "Tap:"
prompts — `any-pointer` would also catch touchscreen laptops used with a
keyboard, a review finding), `touch-wide` = touch + landscape; the pad
shows on narrow windows and every touch screen, and on a sideways touch screen
it docks in the bottom corners (`fixed`, pad left, ✋ right, `z-30`, under
the overlays); the toast stays up top on touch screens so it never covers the
pad. **S14:** Wisp now says the Depths are a cave south of the woods (not a
stair under them), and Hazel says Ember's Breath comes when all four crystals
are home. **S6 (part):** the menu's cloud-save warnings drop the raw error
(now a `title`) and "apply the latest database migrations". **B7 (part):**
`index.html` has the title "Hazel Quest", a description and a theme colour.
**Review fix:** the results screen used up the next round's warm-up — the
new level re-keyed the hook, whose fetch took the batch `finishRound` had just
prefetched (consume-once, `lib/questions.ts`) and threw it away, after the
server had marked it seen. `FinishedRound` now keeps the round's `level`, and
`QuizRound` passes `finished?.level` as the hook's `levelOverride`, so a
finished round asks for nothing new.
